We need to prove strong normalization for STLC CBV with locally nameless syntax. Let me think about the approach.

Standard approach: logical relation / reducibility.

Define a unary logical relation `R T t` meaning t is strongly normalizing (for base type Bool) or something stronger for arrow types.

But for CBV with open terms and locally nameless... The key challenge: we need value-based logical relation for CBV? Actually for strong normalization of STLC under CBV, we can use a Girard-style reducibility candidate with the "neutral/sN" approach, or simpler, for simply-typed lambda calculus with CBV, we can use a logical predicate:

- `R Bool t := strongly_normalizing t` (or `sn t ∧ (value t → ...)`) 
- `R (T1 -> T2) t := strongly_normalizing t ∧ forall v, value v → R T1 v → R T2 (app t v)`.

Wait, for CBV, the standard "reducibility candidates" definition: a term t is reducible at arrow type iff t is SN (in the CBV sense, i.e., all reduction paths finite) and for every reducible value v at T1, app t v is reducible at T2. That's the standard.

Then we need: fundamental theorem: for any well-typed term with context Gamma, and any closing substitution (environment) mapping variables to terms reducible at the types in Gamma, the substituted term is reducible at T. And reducible implies SN.

Challenges:
1. The syntax is locally nameless with named free variables (fvar). The typing rules use open with fvar x for the abstraction body. We need substitution for free variables: `subst x u t` replacing fvar x with u. Or we could use an "environment" mapping atoms to terms, and define subst_env.

Actually, since fvars are named and binding does not capture fvars (open only replaces bvars), substitution is straightforward: define `subst x u t` recursively replacing tm_fvar y with u when x = y.

Wait, but there's a subtlety: opening with fvar x — free variable names must be fresh. Standard locally nameless: when opening the abstraction body with a fresh name x, and substituting.

2. The well-typedness of a substituted term: need a substitution lemma. Since has_type uses contexts as functions atom -> option ty, we can prove: if Gamma |-- t \in T and for all x, Gamma x = Some S implies Delta |-- sigma x \in S, then Delta |-- subst sigma t \in T. But with locally nameless opening, the T_Abs rule opens with fresh x; the substitution lemma needs to commute substitutions with opening (subst and open). Standard but a bit fiddly.

Alternatively, we can avoid the general substitution lemma by defining the logical relation on open terms with a context of free variables, i.e., define:

`R T t` for open terms as: for all closing substitution sigma (mapping all free vars in t ... ) hmm.

Alternative approach that avoids the "opening/substitution commutation" difficulty for fvars: define a logical relation semantically over de Bruijn? Hmm.

Another approach: since the language is call-by-value simply-typed lambda calculus, there's a simpler syntactic proof of strong normalization via a "normalization by evaluation"-ish argument, or via a reducibility using `sn` and sizes. Another classic approach: Define a measure-based termination proof? STLC CBV termination can be proven via logical relations.

Let me think about the cleanest formalization given locally nameless with named fvars.

Option A: Define the logical relation as a predicate on terms with free variables interpreted by an environment. Specifically:

- Define `subst_env (sigma : atom -> tm) t`.
- Define `R T t` (closed-ish terms allowed; t may have fvars) inductively:
  - `R Bool t := strongly_normalizing t`
  - `R (T1 -> T2) t := strongly_normalizing t /\ forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v)`

But then the fundamental theorem needs substitution: Gamma |-- t \in T, and a "good environment" sigma such that for all x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x), then R T (subst_env sigma t).

For T_Var: subst_env sigma (fvar x) = sigma x, fine.
For T_App: need R T2 (app (subst t1) (subst t2)); from R (T1->T2) (subst t1) need SN of subst t2 and R T1 (subst t2)... wait, R arrow requires: SN t1 and for all values v reducible at T1, R T2 (app t1 v). We only know R T1 (subst t2) (which is not necessarily a value). Need: if R T1 t2 then SN t2, and if t2 -->* v with value v, then R T1 v? Hmm, that's the "backward closure" / "CR" property: reducibility at T1 is closed under reduction from SN terms? We need: R T1 t2 implies there exists value v with t2 -->* v and R T1 v (for CBV, a reducible term of arrow type need not reduce to a value? Actually SN and... a term of type arrow isn't necessarily going to reduce to a value if it's stuck, but well-typed closed terms don't get stuck. Hmm, but our logical relation is on arbitrary terms).

Standard approach for CBV: Define reducibility candidates for CBV as sets of terms closed under expansion and such that every term in candidate is SN. Then prove the fundamental theorem with the substitution environment. For the application case: given t1 ∈ R(T1->T2) and t2 ∈ R T1, need app t1 t2 ∈ R T2. For call-by-value, the standard candidate definition for arrow type is:

R(T1->T2) = { t | SN(t) ∧ ∀ v, value v ∧ v ∈ R T1 → app t v ∈ R T2 }? 

Then app t1 t2 where t2 needn't be a value. But if t2 is SN, then by induction on SN of t2 (and t1) we can show app t1 t2 ∈ R T2: consider the CBV reduction of app t1 t2: either t1 steps, or t1 is a value and t2 steps, or both are values and beta. Hmm, that's the standard "application closure lemma": if t1 ∈ R(T1->T2) (in particular SN) and t2 ∈ R T1, then app t1 t2 ∈ R T2, proven by induction on the SN derivations. Actually the candidate definition typically includes: t ∈ R(A->B) iff t ∈ SN and ∀u ∈ R A (value u), app t u ∈ R B. Since R A only contains SN terms (by definition of candidate), and R(A->B) contains SN terms. To show app t u ∈ R B, we need SN(app t u) and for R B being base type just SN. For higher B, similar.

SN(app t u): t and u SN. Let me recall the standard reducibility proof for CBV STLC. This is from Pierce's TAPL chapter on normalization, or from "Semantics of types" etc. Let's think concretely.

Claim 2 (fundamental theorem with substitution): if Gamma |-- t ∈ T and sigma closes Gamma with sigma x ∈ R_{Gamma x}, then subst sigma t ∈ R_T.

Definitions:
```
Definition SN := strongly_normalizing.
Definition sn (t : tm) := strongly_normalizing t.
```

Define:
```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => sn t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => sn t /\ (forall v, value v -> LR T1 v -> LR T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.
```

Definitions:
```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => strongly_normalizing t /\ forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u)
  end.
```
Note: quantify over all u ∈ LR T1 (not just values).

Let me define:
```
Fixpoint subst (x : atom) (u : tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else tm_fvar y

  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst x u t1)
  | ...
  end.
```

OK. Now, there's also the typing substitution lemma needed for T_Abs? Let's structure the fundamental theorem directly with environments, avoiding a separate typing substitution lemma:

Theorem fundamental: forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> forall sigma, env_ok sigma Gamma -> LR T (subst_env sigma t).

Where env_ok sigma Gamma := forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> LR S (sigma x).

Proceed by induction on the typing derivation.

- T_Var: Gamma x = Some T. subst_env sigma (fvar x) = sigma x. env_ok gives LR T (sigma x). ✓.

- T_True/T_False: subst_env sigma true = true. Need LR Bool true: lc_at 0 true ∧ sn true. ✓.

- T_App: IH1: LR (T1->T2) (subst_env sigma t1); IH2: LR T1 (subst_env sigma t2). Need LR T2 (subst_env sigma (app t1 t2)) = LR T2 (app (subst_env sigma t1) (subst_env sigma t2)). From arrow definition: second component with u := subst_env sigma t2 gives exactly it. ✓ (Needs the definitional equality to compute; subst_env on app reduces.) 

- T_Abs: need LR (T1->T2) (subst_env sigma (abs T1 t1)) = LR (T1->T2) (abs T1 (subst_env sigma t1)).
  Components: lc_at 0 (abs T1 s1) where s1 = subst_env sigma t1; sn (abs ...); and ∀ u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 s1) u).

Induction on a Prop derivation with `induction 1` gives IH about the immediate subderivations (which are sn u' for reducts). Let's recall: for an inductive Prop like
```
Inductive sn : tm -> Prop := sn_intro : (forall t', step t t' -> sn t') -> sn t.
```
`induction H` where H : sn u gives, in the sn_intro case, the induction hypothesis `forall t', step u t' -> P t'` if the motive is P t := ...? Coq's induction on Prop with a premise that is a function type: The induction principle generated for sn is:
```
sn_ind : forall P : tm -> Prop,
  (forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> sn t') -> (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t) ->
  forall t, sn t -> P t
```
Wait, Coq generates induction principles only for inductive types where recursive occurrences appear in strictly positive positions; here sn occurs in the premise `(forall t', step t t' -> sn t')` which is positive, so the induction principle has the IH `forall t', step t t' -> P t'`. Yes! Coq does generate sn_ind with IHs for each recursive occurrence, even under a forall. Let me recall: For `Inductive sn : tm -> Prop := sn_intro : forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> sn t') -> sn t.`, the generated principle:
```
sn_ind : forall P : tm -> Prop,
  (forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> sn t') -> (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t) ->
  forall t, sn t -> P t
```
Yes, I'm fairly confident Coq handles nested recursive occurrences in forall premises this way (like Acc). Acc's induction principle is exactly that. So `induction Hsn` yields IH : forall t', step u t' -> P t' where P is the motive we choose. 

So we can do induction on Hsn : strongly_normalizing u with motive P u := LR T1 u -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) u) (with T1,T2,body fixed). But our lemma needs to work for arbitrary u without assuming sn; we have Hu giving sn. Let's write:

Hstep : forall t', step u t' -> sn t'
IH : forall t', step u t' -> LR T1 t' -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) t')
Hu : LR T1 u
============================
LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) u)
```
That's exactly what we want.

Let's look at how the actual literature proves SN for CBV. I recall the "reducibility" definition for CBV STLC from the book "The formal semantics of programming languages" or from a paper on normalization:

Actually here's an approach: For CBV, the crucial property is that the argument is a value when beta fires. We can define the logical relation over *values* for the arrow domain:

Define two families:
- `V_T(v)`: v is a value of type T and "reducible as a value".
- `R_T(t)`: t is SN and, if t evaluates to a value v, then V_T(v). But "evaluates to a value" needs the big-step relation.

Since step is deterministic, define big-step evaluation relation `eval : tm -> tm -> Prop` as reflexive-transitive closure ending in a value:
```
Inductive eval : tm -> tm -> Prop :=
  | eval_value : forall v, value v -> eval v v
  | eval_step : forall t t' v, t --> t' -> eval t' v -> eval t v.
```
Then define:
```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Bool => v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false   (* or value v with v=true/false *)
  | Arrow T1 T2 => value v /\ forall u, VR T1 u -> R T2 (app v u)  (* u value *)
  end
with R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strong... 
```

ST_App2 needs value t; but if t is a value and doesn't step, that's possible (t = abs with no steps). Wait, "stuck" should mean t is not a value and has no step? If t is a value abstraction, app t u can beta when u is a value. So define neutral/stuck as: t has no step AND t is not a value. Then app t u: ST_App1 ✗ (t no step), ST_App2 ✗ (t not value), ST_AppAbs ✗ (t not abs... t not value). So app t u stuck. And lc (u lc from LR). So by IH on T2: app t u ∈ LR T2. ✓.

Great, so we have:
Lemma LR_stuck: forall T t, lc_at 0 t -> (forall t', ~ step t t') -> ~ value t -> LR T t.

This handles the stuck u case: if u is not a value and has no step, then app (abs T1 body) u is stuck (abs is a value so ST_App1 ✗; ST_App2 needs u step ✗; ST_AppAbs needs value u ✗). So it's stuck lc (abs lc and u lc) and not a value → LR T2 by LR_stuck. ✓

Lemma LR_step_back: forall T X Y, lc_at 0 X -> step X Y -> LR T Y -> LR T X.

T_Var: subst_env sigma (fvar x) = sigma x. Is this definitional? Define subst_env as a Fixpoint:
```
Fixpoint subst_env (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_env sigma t1) (subst_env sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_env sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.
```
Then subst_env sigma (tm_fvar x) = sigma x definitionally ✓.

T_True/False: subst_env sigma tm_true = tm_true. Need LR Bool tm_true: lc ∧ sn. lc_at 0 true = lc_true. sn: SN_intro with no steps; prove `sn_true`, `sn_false`, `sn_abs`, `sn_bvar`, `sn_fvar`: any term that never steps is SN. Actually more generally `sn_of_no_step`.

Given: T_Abs with L, Gamma, T1, T2, t1, Hbody : forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2. The premise is a quantified statement `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...`; Coq's induction on has_type gives an IH of type `forall x, ~ In x L -> <motive applied to the premise's conclusion>`, because the recursive occurrence appears under the forall x and implication. So IH : forall x, ~In x L -> forall sigma, env_ok sigma (update Gamma x T1) -> LR T2 (subst_env sigma (open t1 (fvar x))). Good.

  Hbody_inst := Hbody x0 HnotinL.

So the plan works. We also need the Abs-App lemma as a standalone proven using SN induction, backward closure, CR2, LR_stuck. Let's re-examine the Abs-App lemma proof in detail, especially its hypothesis needs:

Lemma abs_app_LR: forall T1 T2 (body : tm),
  lc_at 1 body ->   (* lc(abs T1 body) *)
  (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (open body u)) ->
  forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).

Proof: intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody u Hu. We have Hsn := LR_sn T1 u Hu. Revert u Hu? Let's do:
  revert u Hu.
  induction Hsn as [u Hstep IH]. 
Hmm, `induction Hsn` with Hsn : sn u where u is in context... We want motive P := fun u => LR T1 u -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) u). Doing `induction Hsn` should revert u. But Hsn was obtained as LR_sn T1 u Hu after intros; we need to be careful about the dependency. Better structure the lemma as:

Lemma abs_app_LR_aux : forall T1 T2 body,
  lc_at 1 body ->
  (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (open body u)) ->
  forall u, strongly_normalizing u -> LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody u Hsn.
  induction Hsn as [u Hstep IH].
  intros Hu.
  (* goal: LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) u) *)
  destruct (classic (value u)) as [Hv | Hnv].
  - (* value case *)
    (* LR T2 (open body u) by Hbody Hu *)
    (* step app abs u --> open body u via ST_AppAbs Hlc Hv *)
    (* backward closure *)
    apply LR_step_back with (Y := open body u); [lc(app abs u) | step | Hbody u Hu]
  - destruct (classic (exists u', step u u')) as [[u' Hsu] | Hnostep].
    + (* step case: app abs u --> app abs u' via ST_App2; value(abs) needs lc_at 1 body → value (tm_abs T1 body) via v_abs (locally_closed = lc_at 0 (abs ...)). *)
      apply LR_step_back with (Y := tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u').
      * lc(app abs u)
      * apply ST_App2; [apply v_abs; exact Hlc | exact Hsu]
      * apply IH; [exact Hsu | apply LR_step T1 Hu Hsu]  -- IH : forall t', step u t' -> LR T1 t' -> LR T2 (app abs t')
    + (* stuck: u not value, no step *)
      apply LR_stuck; [lc(app abs u) | no step | not value]
      
Hmm wait: in the value case, u is a value and LR T1 u; we do NOT need Hbody's premise about value—Hbody holds for all LR T1 u. ST_AppAbs requires locally_closed (tm_abs T1 body) (from Hlc) and value u. ✓.

In the step case, IH from induction on Hsn: when `induction Hsn` with Hsn : strongly_normalizing u, the generated IH should be `forall t', step u t' -> LR T1 t' -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) t')` — but wait, the motive P u := LR T1 u -> LR T2 (...), and the IH is `forall t', step u t' -> P t'`. So yes.

But careful: `induction Hsn` after `intros ... u Hsn` leaves goal with u and Hsn; Coq will revert u (and Hu if introduced). Our statement has Hsn as a separate argument so reverting u makes the goal `forall u, sn u -> LR T1 u -> ...`? Hmm, induction Hsn with Hsn : sn u where u is a local variable: Coq's `induction Hsn` will abstract u. The motive will be fun u => sn u -> LR T1 u -> LR T2 (...)? Because after reverting u, Hsn's type depends on u, so both u and Hsn get reverted; then induction on the sn hypothesis... Actually, in practice, `induction Hsn` where Hsn : P u and context has u: Coq replaces occurrences of u in the goal by the induction variable and reverts Hsn? The standard behavior: `induction H` will revert variables that H depends on if the goal depends on them, producing a motive over those variables. Then the case goal will have Hsn reintroduced? Let me just write it as:

```
intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody u Hsn.
remember Hsn as Hsn'. (* maybe unnecessary *)
induction Hsn as [u Hstep IH].
```
Hmm. A robust common pattern:

```
Lemma abs_app_LR_aux : forall T1 T2 body,
  lc_at 1 body ->
  (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (open body u)) ->
  forall u, strongly_normalizing u -> LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody u Hsn.
  revert u Hsn.
  fix IHsn 1. ...
```
Alternatively use well-founded induction on step via Acc:
```
Lemma SN_Acc : forall t, strongly_normalizing t -> Acc step t.
Lemma Acc_SN : ...
```
Then `induction (SN_Acc u Hsn) as [u _ IHacc]`, where IHacc : forall y, step u y -> Acc step y. Then use `IHacc u' Hsu` to get Acc for u', and via Acc_SN get sn u'. But then to use the lemma we'd need to recurse on the lemma itself with a decreasing Acc argument — we can define the lemma by well-founded induction using `well_founded_ind`:

```
Lemma abs_app_LR_aux : forall T1 T2 body, lc_at 1 body -> (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (open body u)) ->
  forall u, strongly_normalizing u -> LR T1 u -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) u).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody.
  (* well-founded induction on the SN relation *)
  enough (H : forall u, strongly_normalizing u -> LR T1 u -> LR T2 (...)) by auto.

Lemma LR_neutral_intro : forall T (t : tm),
  lc_at 0 t ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> LR T t') ->

   Good.

Let's prove LR_step_back:
Lemma LR_step_back : forall T X Y, lc_at 0 X -> step X Y -> LR T Y -> LR T X.
Induction on T.
- Bool: unfold. split: lc X ✓; sn X. Need: sn Y given (from LR Bool Y). sn X via SN_intro: for all Z, step X Z → sn Z. By determinism, Z = Y, rewrite, sn Y. So need step_det.
- Arrow T1 T2: unfold; split lc; split sn (same determinism argument: need for all Z, step X Z -> sn Z; use step_det to get Z = Y and LR_sn (T1->T2) Y); intros u Hu: need LR T2 (app X u). We have LR T2 (app Y u) from LR arrow Y applied to u. And step (app X u) (app Y u) via ST_App1 with lc u. Then apply IH_T2 with lc(app X u): lc X ∧ lc u. ✓.
  Wait: IH_T2 is `forall X Y, lc X -> step X Y -> LR T2 Y -> LR T2 X`. Use X := app X u, Y := app Y u. step (app X u) (app Y u) via ST_App1 Hstep (lc u). ✓.

So backward closure relies on determinism only for the SN component. Alternatively, we could avoid determinism by noting in the SN component: t' with step X t'; can we show sn t' differently? We know LR T Y and step X Y; for arbitrary reduct t', no relation to Y without determinism. So determinism needed.

Let me now double check the SN component for Bool: sn X requires all reducts sn. With step_det: step X Z -> Z = Y -> sn Y. We have sn Y from LR Bool Y. ✓.

Now, forward closure (CR2):
Lemma LR_step : forall T X Y, LR T X -> step X Y -> LR T Y.
Induction on T.
- Bool: LR Bool X = lc X ∧ sn X. Need lc Y ∧ sn Y.
  lc Y: step_lc_pres H X lc.
  sn Y: SN_intro inversion? Prove lemma sn_step: sn X -> step X Y -> sn Y by inversion on sn X: SN_intro gives all reducts sn, so sn Y. ✓.
- Arrow: LR arrow X gives lc X, sn X, ∀u. Need lc Y (step_lc_pres), sn Y (sn_step), and ∀u ∈ LR T1, LR T2 (app Y u). We have LR T2 (app X u) and step (app X u) (app Y u) via ST_App1 Hstep (lc u from LR_lc T1 u). Then IH_T2 gives LR T2 (app Y u). ✓.

Now, let's carefully write the fundamental theorem statement:

```
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_ok sigma Gamma -> LR T (subst_env sigma t).

Induction on the typing derivation. Hmm, when doing `induction 1` or `induction Hty`, the motive must be `fun Gamma t T H => forall sigma, env_ok sigma Gamma -> LR T (subst_env sigma t)`. But the T_Abs case has a recursive occurrence under a forall x and implication; Coq's induction principle gives the IH: `forall x, ~ In x L -> (forall sigma, env_ok sigma (update Gamma x T1) -> LR T2 (subst_env sigma (open t1 (fvar x))))`. Is that right? The induction principle generated for has_type: since the recursive occurrence appears in the premise `(forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2)`, Coq generates:

```
has_type_ind :
 forall P : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop,
 (forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> P Gamma (tm_fvar x) T) ->
 (forall L Gamma T1 T2 t1,
    (forall x, ~ In x L -> P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) ->
    P Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2)) ->
 ...
```

Hmm, does Coq include the IH for a recursive occurrence under a binder? I believe yes: it generalizes recursive occurrences; for `forall x, ~In x L -> has_type ...`, the recursive occurrence is under a product; Coq's induction principle should give `forall x, ~In x L -> P ...`. Let me recall examples: This pattern is exactly the locally nameless T_Abs rule from SF's "Stlc" chapter; in SF, they do `induction Hty` for such rules and it works. Actually, hmm, for mutual/nested occurrences under binders, Coq sometimes fails to generate the expected IH ("cannot find the elimination combinator" or the IH is weakened). I recall that for rules like:
```
| T_Abs : forall x T1 t1, has_type ... -> has_type ...
```
it's fine. For `forall L, (forall x, x \notin L -> has_type ...) -> has_type ...`, the induction principle generation might be problematic because the recursive occurrence is under two binders (forall x and the arrow), but it is strictly positive. Coq should generate the IH as `forall x, ~ In x L -> P ...`. I'm fairly confident this works (SF uses this exact style and proves preservation by induction on typing, relying on such IHs).

Hmm, wait. In SF's Stlc with locally nameless (the `Stlc` chapter, `has_type` with T_Abs : forall L, (forall x, x \notin L -> ...) -> ...), they prove `preservation` by `induction Hty; ...` and the T_Abs case indeed works with the IH `forall x, x \notin L -> has_type ... -> ...`. Yes.

But here the premise's IH will be P applied to the opened term. With P Gamma t T := forall sigma, env_ok sigma Gamma -> LR T (subst_env sigma t). So IH : forall x, ~ In x L -> forall sigma, env_ok sigma (update Gamma x T1) -> LR T2 (subst_env sigma (open t1 (fvar x))). ✓.

Now, let's write the proof of the Abs case in detail:

```
- (* T_Abs *)
  (* goal: forall sigma, env_ok sigma Gamma -> LR (T1 -> T2) (subst_env sigma (tm_abs T1 t1)) *)
  intros sigma Hok.
  simpl.  (* subst_env sigma (tm_abs T1 t1) = tm_abs T1 (subst_env sigma t1) *)
  (* choose fresh x *)
  destruct (fresh_atom (L ++ fvars t1)) as [x Hx].
  (* Hx : ~ In x (L ++ fvars t1) *)
  assert (HxL : ~ In x L) by (intro H; apply Hx; apply in_or_app; left; exact H).
  assert (Hxt : ~ In x (fvars t1)) by (intro H; apply Hx; apply in_or_app; right; exact H).
  assert (Hbody := H x HxL).  (* has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2 *)
  (* IH for that *)
  (* lc *)
  assert (Hlc_t1 : lc_at 1 t1).
  { apply open_lc_0 with (x := x). apply typing_lc with (Gamma := update Gamma x T1) (T := T2). exact Hbody. }
  (* Actually need open_lc_iff : lc_at 0 (open t1 (fvar x)) <-> lc_at 1 t1 *)
  assert (Hlc_s : lc_at 0 (subst_env sigma t1)).
  { apply (proj1 ??) ... using env lc and Hlc_t1: from lc_at 1 t1, subst_env preserves lc_at 1. }
  split.
  * (* lc *) apply lc_abs. exact Hlc_s.
  * split.
    ** (* sn *) apply sn_abs.
    ** (* app condition *) intros u Hu.
       (* use abs_app_LR with body := subst_env sigma t1 *)
       apply (abs_app_LR T1 T2 (subst_env sigma t1) Hlc_s).
       intros u0 Hu0.
       (* Need LR T2 (open (subst_env sigma t1) u0) *)
       (* Apply IH Hbody with sigma0 = env_update sigma x u0 *)
       assert (Hok0 : env_ok (env_update sigma x u0) (update Gamma x T1)).
       { intros y S HS. unfold env_update, update in *.
         destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
         - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. inversion HS. subst S. exact Hu0.
         - apply Hok. exact HS. }
       specialize (H x HxL (env_update sigma x u0) Hok0).
       (* H : LR T2 (subst_env (env_update sigma x u0) (open t1 (fvar x))) *)
       (* rewrite commutation *)
       rewrite (subst_env_open sigma x u0 t1 HxL Hxt (env_lc ...)) in H.
       ...
```

Wait, in the "app condition" of the arrow LR, the variable is u with Hu : LR T1 u. Then abs_app_LR's hypothesis is ∀u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (open body u). We're currently in the proof of that: `intros u Hu`, then apply abs_app_LR with HbodyLR := ... Actually the structure:

Goal: LR (T1->T2) (tm_abs T1 s) = lc ∧ sn ∧ (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (app (tm_abs T1 s) u)).
We can `repeat split` and then `intros u Hu` and apply abs_app_LR with Hbody := (fun u Hu => ...). So we should prove the Hbody statement first for all u0, then apply abs_app_LR.

```

Where abs_app_LR : forall T1 T2 body, lc_at 1 body? Hmm, we defined it with hypothesis Hlc_body (lc of the abs) and HbodyLR. In the application, body := s and Hlc_s : lc_at 0 s = lc_at 0 (subst...). And in the T_Abs lc part: goal lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 s), which by constructor is lc_at 1 s. ✓.

Now for the commutation rewrite: the IH gives LR T2 (subst_env sigma0 (open t1 (fvar x))). The commutation lemma says subst_env sigma0 (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst_env sigma t1) u0 = open s u0. So rewrite.

But careful: the commutation lemma requires sigma0 = env_update sigma x u0, and the lc conditions:
- (forall y, lc_at 0 (env_update sigma x u0 y)): for y = x, u0 lc (from LR_lc T1 u0 Hu0); else sigma y lc (from env_ok sigma Gamma + LR_lc: Hok y S HS gives LR S (sigma y), hence lc).
- x ∉ fvars t1.
Additionally, does the commutation lemma require anything about u0 lc? In the identity, no. But we need the "env lc" for sigma0, which includes u0 lc at x. Fine.

Hmm wait, actually let's double check the commutation lemma's fvar case for y = x is excluded by freshness only if x ∉ fvars t1. Yes.

Great.

Now, what about the `env_ok` condition requiring Gamma x = Some T → LR T (sigma x); in the T_Var case, the derivation has Gamma x = Some T and subst_env sigma (fvar x) = sigma x; so exact Hok x T H. ✓.

Now, the T_App case: goal after simpl: LR T2 (tm_app (subst_env sigma t1) (subst_env sigma t2)). We have IH1 sigma Hok : LR (T1->T2) (subst_env sigma t1) and IH2 sigma Hok : LR T1 (subst_env sigma t2). Then the arrow condition: `apply (proj2 (proj2 IH1))`? Let's define LR arrow as a conjunction; better to write helper lemmas:

```
Lemma LR_arrow_app : forall T1 T2 t u, LR (T1->T2) t -> LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u).
Lemma LR_arrow_sn : ...
```

Actually, since LR is a Fixpoint with match, `simpl` on LR (T1->T2) t reduces to `lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\ forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u)`. Let's define the shape consistently. Choose:
```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```
Note the arrow: `A /\ B /\ C` parses as `A /\ (B /\ C)`. So proj1 = lc, proj1 (proj2) = sn, proj2 (proj2) = app condition. Fine; helper lemmas make it clean.

Helper:
```
Lemma LR_lc : forall T t, LR T t -> lc_at 0 t.
Proof. intros T; induction T; intros t H; simpl in H; [apply (proj1 H) | apply (proj1 H)]. Qed.
```
Hmm, `induction T` then `destruct H` works: for Bool, H : lc ∧ sn; arrow H : lc ∧ (sn ∧ ...). `exact (proj1 H)`. Since LR's match reduces definitionally, `destruct H as [Hlc _]` after `simpl in H`. Actually `simpl in H` might reduce LR (Ty_Bool) t to the conjunction. Then proj1.

```
Lemma LR_sn : forall T t, LR T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros T; induction T; intros t H; simpl in H; [apply (proj2 H) | apply (proj1 (proj2 H))]. Qed.
```
Wait for Bool: H : lc ∧ sn, proj2 H : sn ✓. Arrow: H : lc ∧ (sn ∧ appcond), proj1 (proj2 H) : sn ✓.

```
Lemma LR_app : forall T1 T2 t u, LR (T1->T2) t -> LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u).
Proof. intros T1 T2 t u H Hu. simpl in H. apply (proj2 (proj2 H)) Hu. Qed.
```
Hmm, `simpl in H` may or may not reduce; since LR is a Fixpoint and (T1->T2) is a constructor application, it should reduce.

To construct LR at arrow: `split; [lc | split; [sn | intros ...]]`. With the definition above, `split` on `lc /\ sn /\ app`: first `split` gives goals lc and (sn /\ app); second `split` gives sn and app. Fine.

Lemma In_le_fold_max : forall L x, In x L -> x <= fold_right max 0 L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L IH]; intros x Hx; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hx.
  - destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (fold_right max 0 L); [apply IH; exact Hx | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Now subst_env:

```coq
Definition env := atom -> tm.

Fixpoint subst_env (sigma : env) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_env sigma t1) (subst_env sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_env sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition env_update (sigma : env) (x : atom) (u : tm) : env :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.
```

```coq
(** ** Free variables and freshness *)

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Lemma In_le_fold_max : forall (L : list atom) (x : atom),
  In x L -> x <= fold_right max 0 L.
Proof.
  intros L. induction L as [| a L IH]; intros x Hx; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hx.
  - destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (fold_right max 0 L); [apply IH; exact Hx | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall L : list atom, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof.
  intros L. exists (S (fold_right max 0 L)).
  intros Hx. apply (In_le_fold_max L) in Hx. lia.
Qed.
```
Check: lia should handle S m ≤ m contradiction. ✓

Lemma lc_open : forall k t u, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).

Hidden snag candidate 4: LR_step_back's SN component uses step_det. Need step_det proven. Let's think about step_det proof in more detail.

```
Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2.
Proof.
  intros t t1 t2 H1. revert t2. induction H1 as [T t v Hlc Hv | t1 t1' t2 Hstep IH Hlc | v1 t2 t2' Hv Hstep IH].
```
Hmm, the induction principle for step: 
`step_ind : forall P : tm -> tm -> Prop, (forall T t v, lc(abs) -> value v -> P (app (abs) v) (open t v)) -> (forall t1 t1' t2, step t1 t1' -> P t1 t1' -> lc t2 -> P (app t1 t2) (app t1' t2)) -> (forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> step t2 t2' -> P t2 t2' -> P (app v1 t2) (app v1 t2')) -> forall t t0, step t t0 -> P t t0.`

For determinism, set P t t1 := forall t2, step t t2 -> t1 = t2. Then:
- Case ST_AppAbs: source app (abs T t) v, target open t v. Need forall t2, step (app (abs T t) v) t2 -> open t v = t2. Intro H2; inversion H2.
  - ST_AppAbs T' t' v' Hlc' Hv': source app (abs T' t') v' = app (abs T t) v → T'=T, t'=t, v'=v, target open t' v' = open t v, goal open t v = open t v ✓.
  - ST_App1 t1 t1' t2 Hstep Hlc2: source's first component abs T t = t1 and t1 --> t1' — contradict abs_no_step t1' Hstep.
  - ST_App2 v1 t2 t2' Hv1 Hstep2: source app (abs T t) v = app v1 t2 → v1 = abs T t, t2 = v, and v --> t2' contradict value v has no step.
  So inversion H2 should give these; to discharge contradictions use value_no_step / abs_no_step.
- Case ST_App1: source app t1 t2, target app t1' t2, IH : forall t2', step t1 t2' -> t1' = t2'. Need forall t2'', step (app t1 t2) t2'' -> app t1' t2 = t2''.
  inversion H2:
  - ST_AppAbs: source app (abs...) v = app t1 t2 → t1 = abs..., but Hstep : step t1 t1' contradicts abs_no_step. Actually ST_AppAbs's source is app (abs T t) v; unification t1 = abs T t; then Hstep : step (abs T t) t1' contradicts abs_no_step. ✓
  - ST_App1: t1 --> t1'' and target app t1'' t2; goal app t1' t2 = app t1'' t2; need t1' = t1'' from IH t1'' Hstep2. ✓ (use f_equal).
  - ST_App2: value t1 (as v1) contradicts Hstep : step t1 t1' via value_no_step. Actually v1 = t1 and value v1, so value t1; combined with step t1 t1' → contradiction. ✓
- Case ST_App2: source app v1 t2, target app v1 t2', IH : forall t2'', step t2 t2'' -> t2' = t2''. Need forall t2'', step (app v1 t2) t2'' -> app v1 t2' = t2''.
  inversion:
  - ST_AppAbs: source app (abs T t) v = app v1 t2 → v1 = abs..., t2 = v; but Hstep : step t2 t2' and value t2 (since value v) contradicts value_no_step. ✓
  - ST_App1: t1 --> t1'' where t1 = v1; contradiction value v1 no step. ✓
  - ST_App2: v1' t2'' t2''' with value v1', step t2 t2''', target app v1' t2'''; unification v1'=v1, t2'''... Then use IH. ✓

So step_det needs abs_no_step and value_no_step which are simple inversions.

Hidden snag candidate 5: Does `step_lc` need generalization over n? Yes as discussed.

```
Lemma step_lc : forall n t t', step t t' -> lc_at n t -> lc_at n t'.
Proof.
  intros n t t' Hstep. induction Hstep; intros Hlc.
  - (* beta *) inversion Hlc; subst. apply lc_open.
    + assumption.   (* lc_at (S n) body from lc_at n (app (abs) v) -> lc_at n (abs) -> lc_at (S n) body *)
    + assumption.
  ...
```
Hmm, careful: the beta constructor's premise `locally_closed (tm_abs T t)` = lc_at 0 (tm_abs T t); lc_at n (app (abs T t) v) gives lc_at n (abs T t) which via inversion gives lc_at (S n) t. Then lc_open with k = n gives lc_at n (open_rec n v t) = lc_at n (open t v). ✓ Wait: `open t v = open_rec 0 v t`, not open_rec n! For n = 0 it's the same, but for general n, the term t' = open t v = open_rec 0 v t. We need lc_at n (open_rec 0 v t) from lc_at (S n) t and lc_at n v. Use lc_open with k = n?? lc_open : lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t). We need open_rec 0, i.e., k = 0, but then lc_at (S 0) t, which we don't have (we have lc_at (S n) t). Hmm.

Hold on. Let's recompute: lc_at n (app (abs T t) v) gives lc_at n (abs T t) and lc_at n v. lc_at n (abs T t) gives lc_at (S n) t? The lc_abs constructor: lc_at k (tm_abs T t1) from lc_at (S k) t1. So lc_at n (abs T t) → lc_at (S n) t. ✓. And lc_at n v. Need lc_at n (open_rec 0 v t). But lc_open gives lc_at k (open_rec k u t) from lc_at (S k) t and lc_at k u. With k = n: open_rec n, not open_rec 0. Mismatch!

Hmm. So my claim that step preserves lc at arbitrary depth n is wrong as stated? Let's think: t (the body) is under one binder, so relative to depth n for the outer app, the body is at depth n+1. Opening with v at index 0... but when the app is itself at depth n (i.e., under n binders), the free bvars of t (relative to its own depth 1) are already ≥ 1, so opening at 0 only replaces bvar 0 which is the bound variable of the abs, and free bvars of the whole term at depth n correspond to indices ≥ n... Hmm, let's carefully check the semantics of lc_at.

lc_at n t means: all free bvars of t have indices < n, where "free" occurrences are those not bound by binders within t. Example: t = app (abs T (bvar 1)) v. lc_at 0 t requires lc_at 0 (abs T (bvar 1)) = lc_at 1 (bvar 1): 1 < 1 false. So not lc at 0. ```
Definition env_lc (sigma : env) : Prop := forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x).
Definition env_ty (sigma : env) (Gamma : context) : Prop := forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x).
Definition env_ok sigma Gamma := env_lc sigma /\ env_ty sigma Gamma.
```

Then in T_Var: env_ty gives LR T (sigma x) ✓ regardless of lc.
T_Abs: build sigma0 = env_update sigma x u; env_lc holds (x ↦ u lc, others sigma lc); env_ty for update Gamma x T1 holds as before. ✓.

Fundamental theorem statement: forall sigma, env_ok sigma Gamma -> LR T (subst_env sigma t). ✓.

Now, LR_step (forward closure):
```
Lemma LR_step : forall T t t', LR T t -> step t t' -> LR T t'.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t t' Hlr Hst.
  - simpl in Hlr. simpl. destruct Hlr as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + apply sn_step with (t := t); assumption.
  - simpl in Hlr. simpl. destruct Hlr as [Hlc [Hsn Happ]].
    split; [apply step_lc with t; assumption | split; [apply sn_step with t; assumption |]].
    intros u Hu. apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t u).
    + apply Happ. exact Hu.
    + apply ST_App1; [exact Hst | apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact Hu].
Qed.
```
Careful with simplification: `simpl in Hlr` when Hlr : LR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t — should reduce the Fixpoint. But `induction T` and then T1 T2 are variables in the arrow case; `LR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t` reduces by simpl. ✓.

For the Bool case, `simpl in Hlr` reduces `LR Ty_Bool t` to `lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t`. ✓.

Note: `apply step_lc with (t := t)` — step_lc : forall n t t', step t t' -> lc_at n t -> lc_at n t'. We need lc_at 0 t'. So instantiate n := 0. Better state step_lc without n: `step t t' -> lc_at 0 t -> lc_at 0 t'`. Since only n=0 needed. Let's do that: 

```
Lemma step_lc : forall t t', step t t' -> lc_at 0 t -> lc_at 0 t'.
```
Proof by induction on step Hst; in beta case: lc_at 0 (app (abs T t) v) → lc_at 1 t and lc_at 0 v (via inversion); then lc_open 0 t v. ✓.

For App1: Hst : step t1 t1', Hlc : lc_at 0 (app t1 t2) → lc_at 0 t1, lc_at 0 t2; IH Hlc1 : lc_at 0 t1'; goal lc_app t1' t2. ✓.

For App2 similarly.

Now LR_step_back:
```
Lemma LR_step_back : forall T t t', lc_at 0 t -> step t t' -> LR T t' -> LR T t.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t t' Hlc Hst Hlr.
  - simpl in *. destruct Hlr as [Hlc' Hsn']. split.
    + exact Hlc.
    + apply SN_intro. intros z Hsz. assert (z = t') by (eapply step_det; eauto). subst. exact Hsn'.
  - simpl in *. destruct Hlr as [Hlc' [Hsn' Happ']]. split; [exact Hlc | split].
    + apply SN_intro. intros z Hsz. assert (z = t') by (eapply step_det; eauto). subst. exact Hsn'.
    + intros u Hu. apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t u).
      * apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact Hu].
      * apply ST_App1; [exact Hst | apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact Hu].
      * apply Happ'. exact Hu.
Qed.
```
Check: in the Arrow case, goal after split: `forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u)`. We apply IHT2 with t := app t u (and t' := app t' u): IHT2 : forall t t', lc_at 0 t -> step t t' -> LR T2 t' -> LR T2 t. So with t := app t u, t' := app t' u. Provide lc, step, Hlr. The step: app t u --> app t' u via ST_App1 Hst (lc u) ✓. The Hlr: LR T2 (app t' u) from Happ' u Hu ✓.

In the Bool case: `destruct Hlr as [Hlc' Hsn']`; Hsn' : sn t'. Then SN_intro with all reducts z: step t z → z = t' via step_det Hst Hsz → sn z. ✓.

But wait: is `step_det` symmetric order? step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2. Hst : step t t', Hsz : step t z → t' = z. We need z = t'. Use `symmetry` or prove step_det' orientation. We can write `assert (t' = z) by (eapply step_det; eauto); subst; exact Hsn'`. ✓.

Now, one more consideration: LR_step_back's arrow case uses IHT2, the IH at the codomain — structural subterm ✓.

We need case split on value u. Can we decide value? We could use classical `classic (value u)` — requires Classical_Prop (axiom). Avoid. We can instead do inversion on value u? `value u` is a Prop; we can't branch without decidability. But we can use the following trick: we don't need to decide! Use the neutral lemma as fallback:

If u is a value, beta. If not, neutral lemma. We need a case split on (value u) ∨ ~ value u. This is classically true but constructively? For a syntactic inductive Prop like value, decidability is provable by induction on the syntax of u (if u is an app or bvar or fvar, not a value; if abs, value depends on lc_at 0 (abs), which is decidable; true/false yes). So we can prove `value_dec : forall t, {value t} + {~ value t}` — requires deciding lc_at, which is decidable. Proving lc_at decidability by induction on t: bvar i: decide i < k; fvar: left; app: and; abs: recurse; true/false: left. Actually lc_at k t for bvar requires i < k, decidable by Nat.lt_decidable (`Nat.ltb` or `Compare_dec`). We can use `Nat.lt_ge_cases` or `lt_eq_lt_dec`. There's `PeanoNat.Nat.lt_decidable`? Search. Simplify: prove lc_at decidable via a boolean function? We can define:
```
Fixpoint lc_b (k : nat) (t : tm) : bool :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => Nat.ltb i k
  | tm_fvar _ => true
  | tm_app t1 t2 => andb (lc_b k t1) (lc_b k t2)
  | tm_abs _ t1 => lc_b (S k) t1
  | tm_true => true
  | tm_false => true
  end.
Lemma lc_b_true : forall k t, lc_b k t = true <-> lc_at k t.
```
Then value_dec via lc_b on abs.

Alternative: define the lemma `abs_app_LR` by well-founded induction on u and use the neutral-intro lemma for all u, including values! Yes! `tm_app` is never a value regardless of u. So we can always use the neutral lemma! We don't need to split on value u at all!

Let's redo: To prove LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) u):
Apply LR_neutral_intro at T2 with t := app (abs T1 body) u:
- lc_at 0 (app (abs...) u): lc(abs) from Hlc (lc_at 1 body → lc_at 0 (abs)); lc u from LR_lc.
- ~ value (app ...): inversion.
- forall t', step (app (abs...) u) t' -> LR T2 t'. Inversion on the step:
  * ST_AppAbs T0 body0 v Hlc0 Hv: source app (abs T0 body0) v = app (abs T1 body) u → body0 = body, v = u; Hlc0 : locally_closed (abs T1 body) (could differ? Unification gives equality); Hv : value u. Target = open body u. Need LR T2 (open body u) = Hbody u Hu. ✓. (No need for value u in Hbody!)

  * ST_App2 v1 t2 t2' Hv1 Hstep2: source app v1 t2 = app (abs..) u → v1 = abs..; Hstep2 : step u t2'. Target app v1 t2' = app (abs..) t2'. Need LR T2 (app (abs..) t2') from IH: IH t2' Hstep2 : LR T1 t2' -> LR T2 (app abs t2'); need LR T1 t2' from LR_step T1 Hu Hstep2. ✓.
So indeed no value case split needed! Beautiful. The neutral-intro lemma requires "not a value" of the whole term app, which is syntactically true.

So the proof of abs_app_LR:
```
Lemma abs_app_LR : forall T1 T2 body,
  lc_at 1 body ->
  (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (open body u)) ->
  forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody.
  assert (H : forall u, strongly_normalizing u -> LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u)).
  { intros u Hsn. induction Hsn as [u Hstep IH]. intros Hu.
    apply LR_neutral_intro with (T := T2).
    - (* lc *) apply lc_app. apply lc_abs; exact Hlc. apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact Hu.
    - (* all reducts *) intros t' Hst.
      inversion Hst as [T0 b0 v Hlca Hv | a a' b Hsa Hlcb | v1 b b' Hv1 Hsb]; subst.
      + (* beta *) apply Hbody. exact Hu.
      + (* app1 *) exfalso. eapply abs_no_step; eauto.
      + (* app2 *) apply IH.
        * eassumption.  (* step u b' *)
        * apply LR_step with (t := u); assumption.   (* LR_step T1 Hu Hsb *)
    - (* not value *) intros Hv. inversion Hv.
  }
  intros u Hu. apply H.
  - apply LR_sn with (T := T1); exact Hu.
  - exact Hu.
Qed.
```
Careful in the beta case: after inversion, the target t' becomes `open b0 v`. Unification with source `app (tm_abs T1 body) u` gives b0 = body? and v = u? The inversion of `step (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u) t'` against constructor ST_AppAbs with parameters T0 b0 v: Coq unifies tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u = tm_app (tm_abs T0 b0) v, yielding tm_abs T1 body = tm_abs T0 b0 (so T1=T0, body=b0) and u=v, and t' = open b0 v which becomes open body u. After `subst`, the goal LR T2 t' with t' replaced... `inversion ... as [...]` followed by `subst` should rewrite. But the exact naming and behavior of inversion needs testing.

Potential issue: `inversion Hst as [ ... ]` when the target index t' is a variable: inversion will substitute t' := open b0 v in the goal. Then `subst` handles equalities. Let's test.

Also, in the App2 case, the target app v1 b' with v1 = tm_abs T1 body; after subst, goal LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) b'). IH's conclusion is LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) t') where IH : forall t', step u t' -> LR T1 t' -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) t'). With t' := b'. ✓. `apply IH; [exact Hsb | ...]` where Hsb : step u b'. Hmm, after inversion, the hypothesis might be named Hsb. ✓.

And the LR_step: `apply LR_step with (t := u); assumption` — LR_step : forall T t t', LR T t -> step t t' -> LR T t'. Need LR T1 b'. apply LR_step with (T := T1) (t := u) (t' := b'). ✓.

Now, in the not-value case: `intros Hv; inversion Hv.` Since value (tm_app ...) is impossible. ✓.

Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write it:

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_ok sigma Gamma -> LR T (subst_env sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as
    [ Gamma0 x T0 Heq
    | L Gamma0 T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHAbs
    | Gamma0 t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2
    | Gamma0
    | Gamma0 ].
```
Wait, the induction principle's T_Abs case arguments: L, Gamma, T1, T2, t1, then the premise (forall x, ~In x L -> has_type ...), then the IH (forall x, ~In x L -> P ...). So pattern `L Gamma0 T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHAbs`. But note that in the conclusion, the type is written as `T1 -> T2` (Ty_Arrow T1 T2). And the motive P's third argument is the type. Fine.

Hmm, one issue: `induction Hty as [...]` — the motive is implicit `fun Gamma t T _ => forall sigma, env_ok sigma Gamma -> LR T (subst_env sigma t)`. The `as` patterns name the premises. For T_Var: arguments Gamma x T and Heq. For T_App: Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2, then Ht1, IH1, Ht2, IH2. Yes.

But note: the induction principle substitutes the *motive* for the recursive has_type occurrences, so IHAbs : forall x, ~ In x L -> forall sigma, env_ok sigma (update Gamma0 x T1) -> LR T2 (subst_env sigma (open t1 (fvar x))). ✓.

Now, `normalization`:
```
Theorem normalization : forall t T, empty |-- t \in T -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  (* Need an environment sigma with env_ok sigma empty. Since empty always None, env_ty is vacuous; just need env_lc. Choose sigma := fun _ => tm_true. *)
  pose (sigma := fun _ : atom => tm_true).
  assert (Hok : env_ok sigma empty).
  { split.
    - intros x. apply lc_true.
    - intros x S H. unfold empty in H. inversion H. }
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty sigma Hok) as Hlr.
  (* Hlr : LR T (subst_env sigma t) *)
```
But we need strongly_normalizing t, not of subst_env sigma t. Hmm! For the target theorem, t is closed w.r.t. fvars? Not necessarily: `empty |-- t \in T` means all free variables have types in empty, i.e., there are no typed free variables, but t could contain fvars not in the context? has_type T_Var requires Gamma x = Some T; with empty, no fvar can be typed. Could t contain fvars nonetheless? The typing rules: T_Abs opens with fvars, and then those fvars are in the context. A term like `fvar y` cannot be typed with empty. But a term like `true` has no fvars. I think has_type empty t T implies t has no free fvars... Is that true? Consider `tm_abs T (tm_fvar x)`? T_Abs premise: x0 |-> T1 ; empty |-- open (fvar x) (fvar x0) = fvar x \in T2; requires (update empty x0 T1) x = Some T2; if x ≠ x0, empty x = None, contradiction. So no. In general, we can prove: has_type Gamma t T -> forall x, In x (fvars t) -> Gamma x <> None. Then with empty, t has no fvars. Then subst_env sigma t = t (since no fvars). 

So we need a lemma `typing_fvars : has_type Gamma t T -> forall x, In x (fvars t) -> exists S, Gamma x = Some S`. Then with empty, no fvars, and a lemma `subst_env_id : (forall x, ~ In x (fvars t)) -> forall sigma, subst_env sigma t = t`? Hmm, the condition "forall x, ~ In x (fvars t)" is equivalent to fvars t = nil. Then prove subst_env sigma t = t by induction on t when fvars t = nil.

Alternative approach: Instead of proving t has no fvars, we can choose the environment sigma := fun x => tm_fvar x (i.e., identity substitution)! Is the identity environment lc? fvar x is lc_at 0 ✓. And env_ty for empty is vacuous ✓. Then subst_env identity t = t (by induction on t). Then Hlr : LR T t directly. 

But wait: `env_lc` for sigma := fvar requires lc_at 0 (tm_fvar x) ✓ lc_fvar. env_ty for empty: empty x = None, vacuous ✓. And subst_env (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t: prove `subst_env_id : forall t, subst_env (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t` by induction on t. ✓.

Then fundamental gives LR T t, and LR_sn gives strongly_normalizing t. 

Wait, but is that legitimate? env_ok for the identity environment requires LR S (sigma x) for Gamma x = Some S; with Gamma = empty, never. So any lc environment works. Identity is lc. ✓. And subst_env identity t = t regardless of fvars. ✓. Then LR T t for the original t. Since t is well-typed with empty, LR T t holds. But hold on: is LR T t true for t with untyped fvars? Hmm, naming. Let's use `inversion Hst as [...]` with patterns to name hypotheses. Let me write the pattern for step's constructors:

step constructors:
- ST_AppAbs : forall T t v, locally_closed (tm_abs T t) -> value v -> step (tm_app (tm_abs T t) v) (open t v)
- ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, step t1 t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> step (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1' t2)

Alternative: define custom inversion lemmas for step at applications:

```
Lemma step_app_inv : forall t1 t2 t',
  step (tm_app t1 t2) t' ->
  (exists T body v, t1 = tm_abs T body /\ t2 = v /\ t' = open body v) \/
  (exists t1', t1 --> t1' /\ t' = tm_app t1' t2) \/
  (exists v1 t2', t2 --> t2' /\ t' = tm_app v1 t2').
```
Hmm, but we also need side conditions for the cases where they matter (value, lc). We can include them or derive them when needed from the fact that a step constructor was used. If we return existentials without side conditions, we might lose info like `value u` in the beta case; but in abs_app_LR we don't need value u. In LR_neutral_intro's App1 case we need step t t1' (have it) and no side conditions. In the beta case we need t = abs and then contradiction with ~value; the side condition is `locally_closed (tm_abs T body)` which is exactly what we need to conclude value t! Hmm, without it we can't derive value t. But in LR_neutral_intro, the source's first component t unifies with tm_abs T body, and we need Hnv : ~ value t. To contradict, we need value (tm_abs T body), which needs locally_closed. So the side condition IS needed there.

So include side conditions in the inversion lemma:
```
Lemma step_app_inv : forall t1 t2 t',
  step (tm_app t1 t2) t' ->
  (exists T body v, t1 = tm_abs T body /\ t2 = v /\ t' = open body v /\
                    locally_closed (tm_abs T body) /\ value v)
  \/ (exists t1', step t1 t1' /\ t' = tm_app t1' t2 /\ locally_closed t2)
  \/ (exists t2', value t1 /\ step t2 t2' /\ t' = tm_app t1 t2').
```
Proved by `intros; inversion H; subst; eauto 10` perhaps. But the disjunct equality rewriting is a pain. Direct inversion in each usage might be simpler.

```
Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2.
Proof.
  intros t t1 t2 H1. revert t2.
  induction H1 as [T body v Hlc Hv | a a' b Hsa IH Hlcb | v1 b b' Hv1 Hsb IH].
```
Hmm, the step_ind gives P t t0 with the recursive step hypothesis followed by IH. The `as` pattern for ST_AppAbs: `T body v Hlc Hv` (5 args). For ST_App1: `a a' b Hsa IH Hlcb`. For ST_App2: `v1 b b' Hv1 Hsb IH`.

Wait, the order in step_ind: `(forall t1 t1' t2, t1 --> t1' -> P t1 t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> P ...)`, so pattern `a a' b Hsa IH Hlcb` ✓. And ST_App2: `(forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> t2 --> t2' -> P t2 t2' -> P ...)` → `v1 b b' Hv1 Hsb IH` ✓.

Then:
- ST_AppAbs case: goal `forall t2, step (tm_app (tm_abs T body) v) t2 -> open body v = t2`. Intro H2. Inversion H2:
  * ST_AppAbs: gives tm_abs T body = tm_abs T0 body0 and v = v0; target open body0 v0 = open body v ✓ (after subst).
  * ST_App1: source app (abs T body) v = app a1 a2 where a1 --> a1'; need a1 = abs T body, and Hsa : step (abs T body) a1' — contradiction abs_no_step.
  * ST_App2: source app (abs T body) v = app v1 b; v1 = abs T body; Hsb : step v b'; but v is a value (Hv) — contradiction value_no_step.
- ST_App1 case: goal forall t2, step (app a b) t2 -> app a' b = t2. Intro H2; inversion H2:
  * ST_AppAbs: source app a b = app (abs T0 body0) v0 → a = abs T0 body0; Hsa : step a a' = step (abs...) a' contradiction abs_no_step.
  * ST_App1: source app a b = app a1 a2; a1 = a, a2 = b; Hstep : step a a1'; target app a1' b. Need app a' b = app a1' b. IH : forall t2, step a t2 -> a' = t2. So a' = a1'. subst; reflexivity.
  * ST_App2: source app a b = app v1 b2 → v1 = a; Hv1 : value a; Hsa : step a a' contradicts value_no_step.
- ST_App2 case: goal forall t2, step (app v1 b) t2 -> app v1 b' = t2. Intro H2; inversion:
  * ST_AppAbs: source app v1 b = app (abs T0 body0) v0 → v1 = abs T0 body0? Wait, source's function part is abs T0 body0, our function part is v1; so v1 = abs T0 body0. Then Hsb : step b b'; but the beta constructor has value v0, and v0 = b; so b is a value; contradiction value_no_step with Hsb.
  * ST_App1: source app v1 b = app a1 a2 → a1 = v1; Hstep : step v1 a1'; Hv1 : value v1; contradiction value_no_step.
  * ST_App2: source app v1 b = app v1' b2 → v1' = v1, b2 = b; Hstep : step b b2'; target app v1 b2'; IH : forall t2, step b t2 -> b' = t2; so b' = b2'; subst; reflexivity.

Inversion details will determine the exact goal shapes. We'll iterate.

Lemma lc_open_fvar_inv : forall k x t,
  lc_at k (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) -> lc_at (S k) t.

    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. inversion Hk; subst. apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - (* fvar *) apply lc_fvar.
  - (* app *) inversion Hk; subst; apply lc_app; [apply IHt1 | apply IHt2]; assumption.
  - (* abs *) apply lc_abs. apply IHt. simpl. inversion Hk; subst. assumption.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Now, typing_lc. Let me write it with the `as` pattern from has_type_ind shown above:

```
Lemma typing_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> lc_at 0 t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as
    [ Gamma0 x T0 Heq
    | L Gamma0 T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHAbs
    | Gamma0 t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2
    | Gamma0
    | Gamma0 ].
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_abs.
    destruct (fresh_atom L) as [x Hx].
    specialize (IHAbs x Hx).
    apply (lc_open_fvar_inv 0 x t1). exact IHAbs.
  - apply lc_app; assumption.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```
Check the T_Abs conclusion type: the induction principle's conclusion P Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2) but here P is the motive of typing_lc: fun Gamma t T => lc_at 0 t (after the intros, the motive is fun Gamma t T _ => lc_at 0 t). Actually `induction Hty` with goal `lc_at 0 t`: the motive is `fun Gamma t T H => lc_at 0 t`. In the T_Abs case, the target is lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 t1) ✓.

`IHAbs : forall x, ~ In x L -> lc_at 0 (open t1 (fvar x))` ✓. `specialize (IHAbs x Hx)` gives lc_at 0 (open t1 (fvar x)). `lc_open_fvar_inv 0 x t1 : lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1) -> lc_at 1 t1`. Since open t1 (fvar x) = open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1 definitionally, applying should work with `apply (lc_open_fvar_inv 0 x t1); exact IHAbs`. ✓.

Note: Hx : ~ In x L from fresh_atom. ✓.

Now, env definitions and env_ok:

```
Definition env := atom -> tm.

Definition env_lc (sigma : env) : Prop := forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x).

Definition env_ty (sigma : env) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x).

Definition env_ok (sigma : env) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  env_lc sigma /\ env_ty sigma Gamma.

Definition env_update (sigma : env) (x : atom) (u : tm) : env :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.
```

```coq
(** ** Free variables *)

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Lemma In_le_fold_max : forall (L : list atom) (x : atom),
  In x L -> x <= fold_right max 0 L.
Proof.
  intros L. induction L as [| a L IH]; intros x Hx; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hx.
  - destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact Hx | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall L : list atom, exists x : atom, ~ In x L.
Proof.
  intros L. exists (S (fold_right max 0 L)).
  intro Hx. pose proof (In_le_fold_max L _ Hx). lia.
Qed.
```

Wait: `S (fold_right max 0 L)` as an atom. In the proof, after `pose proof`, H : S (fold_right max 0 L) ≤ fold_right max 0 L. lia should derive False. ✓.

lc lemmas as above.

Subst section:

```
Definition env := atom -> tm.

Definition env_lc (sigma : env) : Prop :=
  forall x : atom, lc_at 0 (sigma x).

Definition env_update (sigma : env) (x : atom) (u : tm) : env :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Fixpoint subst_env (sigma : env) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_env sigma t1) (subst_env sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_env sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.
```

subst_env_lc:
```
Lemma subst_env_lc : forall sigma t n,
  env_lc sigma ->
  (lc_at n (subst_env sigma t) <-> lc_at n t).
Proof.
  intros sigma t. induction t; intros n Hlc; simpl.
  - (* bvar *) split; intro H; assumption.
  - (* fvar *) split; intro H.
    + apply lc_fvar.
    + apply lc_mono with (n := 0); [lia | apply Hlc].
  - (* app *) split; intro H.
    + inversion H; subst. apply lc_app; [apply (proj1 (IHt1 n Hlc)) | apply (proj1 (IHt2 n Hlc))]; assumption.
    + inversion H; subst. apply lc_app; [apply (proj2 (IHt1 n Hlc)) | apply (proj2 (IHt2 n Hlc))]; assumption.
  - (* abs *) split; intro H.
    + apply lc_abs. apply (proj1 (IHt (S n) Hlc)). inversion H; subst; assumption.
    + apply lc_abs. apply (proj2 (IHt (S n) Hlc)). inversion H; subst; assumption.
  - (* true *) split; intro H; apply lc_true.
  - (* false *) split; intro H; apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma subst_env_open_rec : forall sigma x u t k,
  env_lc sigma ->
  ~ In x (fvars t) ->
  subst_env (env_update sigma x u) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t)

Now, the LR section.

```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.

Lemma LR_lc : forall T t, LR T t -> lc_at 0 t.
Proof. intros T t H. destruct T; simpl in H; apply (proj1 H). Qed.

Lemma LR_sn : forall T t, LR T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros T t H. destruct T; simpl in H; [apply (proj2 H) | apply (proj1 (proj2 H))]. Qed.

Lemma LR_app : forall T1 T2 t u,
  LR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t -> LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u).
Proof. intros T1 T2 t u H Hu. simpl in H. apply (proj2 (proj2 H)). exact Hu. Qed.
```
Wait, in LR_lc, the goal after destruct T and simpl in H is lc_at 0 t; apply (proj1 H) ✓ in both branches.

Hmm, in the Arrow branch, H : lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall u...). proj1 H : lc_at 0 t ✓.

Lemma LR_step : forall T t t', LR T t -> step t t' -> LR T t'.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t t' Hlr Hst.
  - simpl in Hlr. simpl. destruct Hlr as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + apply sn_step with (t := t); assumption.
  - simpl in Hlr. simpl. destruct Hlr as [Hlc [Hsn Happ]]. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.

ty_ind : forall P : ty -> Prop, P Ty_Bool -> (forall t t0, P t -> P t0 -> P (Ty_Arrow t t0)) -> forall t, P t
```
So the constructor's args in the case: t, t0, IH1, IH2. With `as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]` we bind T1 := t, IHT1 := IH for t, T2 := t0, IHT2 := IH for t0. ✓ (Coq's pattern order is arguments then IHs, in the order of recursive occurrences.) Yes.

Lemma LR_step_back : forall T t t', lc_at 0 t -> step t t' -> LR T t' -> LR T t.

  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t t' Hlc Hst Hlr.

    + exact Hlc.

      assert (t' = z) by (eapply step_det; eassumption).

  - simpl in Hlr. simpl. destruct Hlr as [Hlc' [Hsn' Happ']]. split.
    + exact Hlc.
    + split.
      * apply SN_intro. intros z Hsz.
        assert (t' = z) by (eapply step_det; eassumption).
        subst. exact Hsn'.
      * intros u Hu. apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t u) (t' := tm_app t' u).

        apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t u) (t' := tm_app t' u).
```
If binder names differ, Coq errors. Alternatively, restructure the lemma statement's binder names consistently: `forall T (t t' : tm), ...`. The IHT2 in the induction will have binders named t, t' as in the motive? The motive is generated from the goal after `intros T`; the goal at that point is `forall t t' : tm, lc_at 0 t -> step t t' -> LR T t' -> LR T t`. So the motive's function is exactly that, and IHT2 should be `forall t t', ...`. Since the goal has explicit binder names `t t'`, the motive will use them. I think `apply IHT2 with (t := ...)` works. To be safest, define the lemma with explicit argument order and use positional `apply (IHT2 (tm_app t u) (tm_app t' u))` leaving the rest as goals. Coq's `apply (IHT2 X Y)` will unify conclusion with goal and leave premises as goals. Let's use that style where convenient.

Actually, there's a subtlety: `induction T` when the goal is `forall t t' : tm, ...` — the induction tactic might reorder/hide the forall. It will produce IHT2 with the same type as the motive applied. Fine.

Now, LR_neutral_intro:
```
Lemma LR_neutral_intro : forall T (t : tm),
  lc_at 0 t ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> LR T t') ->
  ~ value t ->
  LR T t.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t Hlc Hreds Hnv.
  - simpl. split.
    + exact Hlc.
    + apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. apply LR_sn with (T := Ty_Bool). apply Hreds. exact Hst.
  - simpl. split.
    + exact Hlc.
    + split.
      * apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. apply LR_sn with (T := Ty_Arrow T1 T2). apply Hreds. exact Hst.
      * intros u Hu.
        apply (IHT2 (tm_app t u)).
        -- apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact Hu].
        -- intros t' Hst. inversion Hst; subst.
           ++ (* ST_AppAbs *) exfalso. apply Hnv. apply v_abs. assumption.
           ++ (* ST_App1 *) apply LR_app with (T1 := T1). 
              ** apply Hreds. assumption.   (* step t t1' *)
              ** exact Hu.
           ++ (* ST_App2 *) exfalso. apply Hnv. assumption.
        -- intros Hv. inversion Hv.
Qed.
```
Note: `apply Hreds` needs Hreds : forall t', step t t' -> LR T t' with T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2. ✓.

Concerns:
1. `inversion Hst; subst` in the reducts proof: the three cases. In the ST_AppAbs case, the goal is `LR T2 t'` where t' is the target open body v; but we do `exfalso; apply Hnv; apply v_abs; assumption` — we need the lc premise from the constructor. After inversion, the premise `locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body)` should be in context. `apply v_abs; assumption` might pick it. But the goal was LR T2 t' — exfalso changes to False; apply Hnv gives goal `value t`; apply v_abs gives goal locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body)? But the goal's type after `apply Hnv` is `value t`; after inversion/subst, does t get replaced by `tm_abs T0 body`? The source app t u = app (tm_abs T0 body) v gives t = tm_abs T0 body; after subst, goal `value (tm_abs T0 body)`. Then `apply v_abs` gives `locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body)`; `assumption` finds the premise. ✓.

2. In the ST_App1 case: after inversion, the source app t u = app t1 t2 gives t = t1, u = t2, and the premise step t1 t1' becomes step t t1'. The goal is LR T2 (app t1' t2) = LR T2 (app t1' u). `apply LR_app with (T1 := T1)` gives goals LR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1' and LR T1 u. First: apply Hreds; assumption (Hreds t1' H). ✓. Second: Hu.

3. In the ST_App2 case: after inversion, the source app t u = app v1 t2 gives v1 = t, so Hv1 : value t; Hnv contradicts. `exfalso; apply Hnv; assumption`. ✓.

But will inversion produce exactly three subgoals with these shapes? Some cases may produce equalities requiring subst. We'll see.

A concern: `inversion Hst; subst` where Hst : step (tm_app t u) t' and t,u are variables; the App1 case's unification t1 = t might not be represented as an equation but directly substituted. Either way, subst handles it.

Another concern: `apply Hreds. assumption.` — Hreds's type at that point: after `inversion Hst; subst`, does Hreds still have the same type `forall t', step t t' -> LR T t'` where T is the arrow type? Yes, since t is unchanged in the App1 case (t1 unifies with t). ✓.

Now abs_app_LR:
```
Lemma abs_app_LR : forall T1 T2 body,
  lc_at 1 body ->
  (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (open body u)) ->
  forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody.
  assert (Haux : forall u, strongly_normalizing u ->
                           LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u)).
  { intros u Hsn. induction Hsn as [u Hstep IH]. intros Hu.
    apply LR_neutral_intro with (T := T2).
    - apply lc_app.
      + apply lc_abs. exact Hlc.
      + apply LR_lc with (T := T1). exact Hu.
    - intros t' Hst. inversion Hst; subst.
      + (* beta *) apply Hbody. exact Hu.
      + (* app1 *) exfalso. eapply abs_no_step; eassumption.
      + (* app2 *) apply IH.
        * eassumption.
        * apply LR_step with (T := T1) (t := u); assumption.
    - intros Hv. inversion Hv.
  }
  intros u Hu. apply Haux.
  - apply LR_sn with (T := T1). exact Hu.
  - exact Hu.
Qed.
```
Concern in the ST_AppAbs case: the goal is LR T2 (open body v) where the constructor's body and v unify with ours; Hbody : forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (open body u). After inversion/subst, the goal should be LR T2 (open body u) — wait, u here is the induction variable and also the term; the constructor's v unifies with u. So `apply Hbody. exact Hu.` ✓.

Concern in ST_App1: Hst source app (abs T1 body) u = app t1 t2 gives t1 = abs T1 body; premise step t1 t1' becomes step (abs T1 body) t1', contradicting abs_no_step. `eapply abs_no_step; eassumption` — abs_no_step : forall T t t', ~ step (tm_abs T t) t'. From eapply, goal False? We're in a goal LR T2 (app t1' u) after inversion, not False. So `exfalso. eapply abs_no_step; eassumption.` ✓.

Concern in ST_App2: premise Hsb : step u t2'; target app (abs T1 body) t2'. IH : forall t', step u t' -> LR T1 t' -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) t'). `apply IH; [eassumption | apply LR_step with ...]`. Hmm, the order: IH t' Hst' Hu'. `apply IH` with goal LR T2 (app abs t2') gives two goals: step u t2' and LR T1 t2'. For the first, eassumption. Second: LR_step with (T := T1) (t := u) (t' := t2') gives goals LR T1 u and step u t2'. Provide Hu and Hsb. `apply LR_step with (T := T1) (t := u); assumption` — the goal is LR T1 t2'; applying LR_step yields goals LR T1 u and step u t2'; assumption solves both (Hu and Hsb). ✓.

But wait: `apply IH` might not work because the IH's conclusion must unify with the goal; IH's conclusion is LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) t'), goal LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) t2') — unifies t' := t2'. ✓.

Now the `inversion Hst` cases order: step constructors order is ST_AppAbs, ST_App1, ST_App2. Inversion should produce subgoals in that order. ✓.

Now env_ty/env_ok definitions after LR:
```
Definition env_ty (sigma : env) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x).

Definition env_ok (sigma : env) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  env_lc sigma /\ env_ty sigma Gamma.
```

typing_lc can be placed before LR (doesn't need it).

Now fundamental:

```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_ok sigma Gamma -> LR T (subst_env sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as
    [ Gamma0 x T0 Heq
    | L Gamma0 T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHAbs
    | Gamma0 t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2
    | Gamma0
    | Gamma0 ].
  - (* T_Var *)
    intros sigma [Hlcs Htys]. simpl. apply Htys. exact Heq.
  - (* T_Abs *)
    intros sigma [Hlcs Htys]. simpl.
    destruct (fresh_atom (L ++ fvars t1)) as [x Hfresh].
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L) by (intro H; apply Hfresh; apply in_app_iff; left; exact H).
    assert (Hxf : ~ In x (fvars t1)) by (intro H; apply Hfresh; apply in_app_iff; right; exact H).
    assert (Hlc_body : lc_at 1 t1).
    { apply (lc_open_fvar_inv 0 x t1).
      apply typing_lc with (Gamma := update Gamma0 x T1) (T := T2).
      apply Hbody. exact HxL. }
    assert (Hlc_s : lc_at 1 (subst_env sigma t1)).
    { apply (proj1 (subst_env_lc sigma t1 1 Hlcs)). exact Hlc_body. }
    split.
    + apply lc_abs. exact Hlc_s.
    + split.
      * apply sn_abs.
      * intros u Hu.
        assert (HbodyLR : forall v, LR T1 v -> LR T2 (open (subst_env sigma t1) v)).
        { intros v Hv.
          assert (Hok0 : env_ok (env_update sigma x v) (update Gamma0 x T1)).
          { split.
            - intros y. unfold env_update. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
              + apply LR_lc with (T := T1). exact Hv.
              + exact (Hlcs y).
            - intros y S Hy. unfold env_update. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
              + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. inversion Hy. subst S. exact Hv.
              + apply Htys. exact Hy. }
          specialize (IHAbs x HxL (env_update sigma x v) Hok0).
          change (LR T2 (subst_env (env_update sigma x v) (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1))) in IHAbs.
          rewrite (subst_env_open_rec sigma x v t1 0 Hlcs Hxf) in IHAbs.
          exact IHAbs. }
        apply (abs_app_LR T1 T2 (subst_env sigma t1) Hlc_s HbodyLR u Hu).
  - (* T_App *)
    intros sigma Hok. simpl.
    apply LR_app with (T1 := T1).
    + apply IH1. exact Hok.
    + apply IH2. exact Hok.
  - (* T_True *)
    intros sigma Hok. simpl. split; [apply lc_true | apply sn_true].
  - (* T_False *)
    intros sigma Hok. simpl. split; [apply lc_false | apply sn_false].
Qed.
```

In T_Abs, the `destruct (fresh_atom (L ++ fvars t1)) as [x Hfresh].` — fresh_atom returns exists x, ~ In x (L ++ fvars t1). ✓.

Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  set (sigma := fun x : atom => tm_fvar x).
  assert (Hid : subst_env sigma t = t).
  { subst sigma. apply subst_env_id. }
  apply LR_sn with (T := T).
  rewrite <- Hid.
  apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty).
  - exact Hty.
  - split.
    + intros x. exact (lc_fvar 0 x).
    + intros x S H. unfold empty in H. discriminate H.
Qed.
```

Now `lc_open_fvar_inv` and `lc_open_fvar` can use lc_open. Let me rewrite lc_open_fvar_inv with the inversion lemmas:

```
Lemma lc_open_fvar_inv : forall k x t,
  lc_at k (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof.
  intros k x t. revert k. induction t; intros k Hk; simpl in Hk.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. apply lc_bvar. lia.

```
Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2.
Proof.
  intros t t1 t2 H1. revert t2.
  induction H1 as
    [ T b v Hlca Hv
    | a a' b Hsa IH Hlcb
    | v1 b b' Hv1 Hsb IH ]; intros t2 H2.
  - (* ST_AppAbs *)
    inversion H2 as [T0 b0 v0 Hlca0 Hv0 | a1 a1' b1 Hsa1 Hlcb1 | v1 b1 b1' Hv1' Hsb1]; subst.
    + reflexivity.
    + exfalso. exact (abs_no_step T b a1' Hsa1).
    + exfalso. exact (value_no_step v b1' Hv Hsb1).
  - (* ST_App1 *)
    inversion H2 as [T0 b0 v0 Hlca0 Hv0 | a1 a1' b1 Hsa1 Hlcb1 | v1 b1 b1' Hv1' Hsb1]; subst.
    + exfalso. exact (abs_no_step T0 b0 a' Hsa).   (* Hsa : a --> a', after a = abs T0 b0 *)
    + f_equal. apply IH. exact Hsa1.
    + exfalso. exact (value_no_step a a' Hv1' Hsa).
  - (* ST_App2 *)
    inversion H2 as [T0 b0 v0 Hlca0 Hv0 | a1 a1' b1 Hsa1 Hlcb1 | v1 b1 b1' Hv1' Hsb1]; subst.
    + exfalso. exact (value_no_step b b' Hv0 Hsb).
    + exfalso. exact (value_no_step v1 a1' Hv1 Hsa1).  (* after v1 = v1 from H1? *)
    + f_equal. apply IH. exact Hsb1.
Qed.
```

Fundamental:
```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_ok sigma Gamma -> LR T (subst_env sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as
    [ Gamma0 x T0 Heq
    | L Gamma0 T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHAbs
    | Gamma0 t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2
    | Gamma0
    | Gamma0 ].
  - (* T_Var *)
    intros sigma [Hlcs Htys]. simpl. exact (Htys x T0 Heq).
  - (* T_Abs *)
    intros sigma [Hlcs Htys]. simpl.
    destruct (fresh_atom (L ++ fvars t1)) as [x Hfresh].
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L).
    { intro H; apply Hfresh; apply in_app_iff; left; exact H. }
    assert (Hxf : ~ In x (fvars t1)).
    { intro H; apply Hfresh; apply in_app_iff; right; exact H. }
    assert (Hlc_body : lc_at 1 t1).
    { apply (lc_open_fvar_inv 0 x t1).
      apply typing_lc with (Gamma := update Gamma0 x T1) (T := T2).
      apply Hbody. exact HxL. }
    assert (Hlc_s : lc_at 1 (subst_env sigma t1)).
    { apply (proj1 (subst_env_lc sigma t1 1 Hlcs)). exact Hlc_body. }
    split.
    + apply lc_abs. exact Hlc_s.
    + split.
      * apply sn_abs.
      * intros u Hu.
        assert (HbodyLR : forall v, LR T1 v -> LR T2 (open (subst_env sigma t1) v)).
        { intros v Hv.
          assert (Hok0 : env_ok (env_update sigma x v) (update Gamma0 x T1)).
          { split.
            - intros y. unfold env_update.
              destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
              + apply LR_lc with (T := T1). exact Hv.
              + apply (Hlcs y).
            - intros y S Hy. unfold env_update.
              destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
              + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. inversion Hy. subst S. exact Hv.
              + apply Htys. exact Hy. }
          specialize (IHAbs x HxL (env_update sigma x v) Hok0).
          change (LR T2 (subst_env (env_update sigma x v) (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1))) in IHAbs.
          rewrite (subst_env_open_rec sigma x v t1 0 Hlcs Hxf) in IHAbs.
          exact IHAbs. }
        exact (abs_app_LR T1 T2 (subst_env sigma t1) Hlc_s HbodyLR u Hu).
  - (* T_App *)
    intros sigma Hok. simpl.
    apply LR_app with (T1 := T1).
    + apply IH1. exact Hok.
    + apply IH2. exact Hok.
  - (* T_True *)
    intros sigma Hok. simpl. split; [apply lc_true | apply sn_true].
  - (* T_False *)
    intros sigma Hok. simpl. split; [apply lc_false | apply sn_false].
Qed.

Theorem normalization : ...
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  apply LR_sn with (T := T).
  rewrite <- subst_env_id.
  apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty).
  - exact Hty.
  - split.
    + intros x. apply lc_fvar.
    + intros x S H. simpl in H. discriminate H.
Qed.
```

Concerns:
1. In T_Var, `intros sigma [Hlcs Htys]` destructs env_ok sigma Gamma0 into env_lc and env_ty. Then goal after simpl is LR T0 (sigma x)? Wait, subst_env sigma (tm_fvar x) = sigma x — simpl unfolds. `exact (Htys x T0 Heq)`. Htys : env_ty sigma Gamma0 = forall x T, Gamma0 x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x). We want to prove lc_at 1 (subst_env sigma t1) from lc_at 1 t1. That's the WRONG direction! We need proj2. Let me recheck: the iff is `lc_at n (subst_env sigma t) <-> lc_at n t`. To prove lc_at n (subst_env sigma t), we use the converse of the forward direction, i.e., proj2: lc_at n t -> lc_at n (subst_env sigma t). So use `apply (proj2 (subst_env_lc sigma t1 1 Hlcs)). exact Hlc_body.` ✓. I wrote proj1; fix.

              destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
              + unfold update in Hy. rewrite E in Hy. inversion Hy. subst S. exact Hv.
              + unfold update in Hy. rewrite E in Hy. apply Htys. exact Hy.

   Hmm, `unfold update in Hy` where update is the supplied Definition `update (Gamma : context) (x : atom) (T : ty) : context := fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else Gamma y`. Lemma env_ok_update : forall sigma Gamma x T v,

```

   Then `apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty).` The fundamental's conclusion: forall sigma, env_ok sigma empty -> LR T (subst_env sigma t).
